  "justificativa": "O defeito 1 nao era x pequeno, era ARITMETICA: um plano em z=-2300 com a camera em 0 projeta com escala 0,36, entao o x de layout encolhe 2,8x em direcao ao centro — a premissa de que x vive entre -860 e +860 so vale para escala 1, e por isso a composicao inteira colapsava no terco do meio. Resolvi ancorando a cena em tres soquetes fixos em x de TELA (420 / 860 / 1330) e resolvendo o x de layout de tras para frente pela projecao em cada beat, o que levou a ocupacao lateral de ~36% para 63,6-68,0% nos beats povoados, e a diagonal do ato final atravessa de 330 a 1429. O defeito 4 tinha duas causas materiais que so apareceram rodando o motor: `cutout` le `w` e ignora `h` (`:166`), entao TODO recorte do EP07 renderiza com os 300px do default — e por isso a caneca e o microfone saem quase do mesmo tamanho; e os PNGs de `pecas/` nao sao aparados, com o centroide de tinta do mic 10,7% fora do centro do canvas que serve de ancora. Corrigi os dois com numero medido e construi a escada 400/340/215/48px (1 : 0,85 : 0,54 : 0,12), que implica 175cm/175cm/82,5cm/17,6cm — uma relacao que fecha. O chao veio de graca da geometria: dei aos dois homens a MESMA altura de layout e a MESMA linha de pes em y de plano, e a profundidade sozinha poe o mais distante 15% menor e com os pes 45px mais altos, acima do horizonte — mais a oclusao da caneca cruzando a base do mic em 2.206px2, que e o unico indicio que mata 'adesivo colado' de vez. Para o foco unico eu parei de torcer pelo desfoque e passei a apoia-lo em existencia e nascimento: a mesa sai inteira as 15,10 para a cifra ficar SOZINHA quando a voz diz a cifra (e por isso ela domina, nao por tamanho contra os chips), e cada chip nasce no seu proprio onset — o resultado medido e dominancia de 2,6x a 41,3x em peso otico (tinta na tela x opacidade / (1+blur)^2), com 4 dos 7 beats tendo o foco literalmente sozinho no quadro. Ao fazer isso descobri que o mapeamento estava trocado: em 27,10 a voz diz 'a audiencia que a PLATAFORMA paga' (ADSENSE) e o foco cai em PATROCINIO, entao remapeei os chips para os soquetes na ordem falada, e a enumeracao virou uma diagonal descendente que le como lista e recupera 260px de altura que a fileira nivelada desperdicava. RECUSO, porem, declarar isto aprovado (meu gate G6): tudo acima e aritmetica sobre a formula de projecao do proprio motor, sem UM probe frame — preciso dos 7 onsets renderizados, do teste de 0,5s e da medicao de densidade de borda antes de assinar; e sinalizo como BLOQUEANTE para o gate ocular um achado que e da Arte e nao meu: `apresentador2-cut.png` esta SEM o contorno branco que `apresentador-cut.png` tem, ou seja, as duas figuras humanas da mesma cena tem tratamento de borda diferente — o que por si so faz uma delas ler como adesivo."

> 285	.pl{position:absolute;left:50%;top:50%;transform-style:preserve-3d;will-change:transform}
> 286	/* coordenadas RELATIVAS AO CENTRO do plano (o .pl e 0x0) — nada de % aqui */
> 287	#horizon{position:absolute;left:-1700px;top:${num(ATMO.horizonY, 380)}px;width:3400px;height:5px;border-radius:50%;
> 288	  background:linear-gradient(90deg,transparent,rgba(0,220,255,.6) 22%,rgba(150,90,255,.62) 62%,transparent);
> 289	  filter:blur(12px)}
> 290	.neb{position:absolute;border-radius:50%;filter:blur(70px);opacity:.34}
> 291	.slab{position:absolute;border-radius:32px;overflow:hidden;background:#080b14;
> 292	  border:1px solid rgba(150,120,255,.32);
> 293	  box-shadow:0 44px 96px rgba(0,0,0,.8), 0 0 54px rgba(124,72,255,.24);
> 294	  will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 295	.slab img,.slab video{width:100%;height:100%;object-fit:cover;display:block;opacity:.95}
> 296	.slab .sheen{position:absolute;inset:0;pointer-events:none;
> 297	  background:linear-gradient(102deg, rgba(255,255,255,.20) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,.03) 20%, transparent 44%)}
> 298	.slab .rim{position:absolute;inset:0;border-radius:32px;pointer-events:none;
> 299	  box-shadow:inset 0 0 70px rgba(124,72,255,.34), inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,.20)}
> 300	.tag{position:absolute;left:20px;bottom:18px;font-size:13px;letter-spacing:.17em;text-transform:uppercase;
> 301	  color:#cdeeff;opacity:.85;font-weight:600;text-shadow:0 2px 10px #000}
> 302	/* ancoragem por CENTRO nos kinds de largura intrinseca — o mesmo contrato dos kinds com w/h */
> 303	.cutwrap,.chip,.badge,.counter,.stamp{transform:translate(-50%,-50%)}
> 304	.cutwrap{position:absolute;will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 305	.cutwrap img{display:block;
> 306	  filter:drop-shadow(0 24px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.9)) drop-shadow(0 0 30px rgba(0,215,255,.30))}
> 307	.cutwrap.noglow img{filter:drop-shadow(0 24px 30px rgba(0,0,0,.9))}
> 308	.slab.plain{background:none;border:none;box-shadow:0 34px 80px rgba(0,0,0,.7)}
> 309	.slab.plain img,.slab.plain video{opacity:1}
> 310	.chip{position:absolute;display:flex;align-items:center;gap:12px;padding:14px 22px 14px 15px;
> 311	  border-radius:17px;background:rgba(16,10,34,.80);border:1.5px solid rgba(178,120,255,.9);
> 312	  box-shadow:0 0 30px rgba(140,70,255,.55), inset 0 0 26px rgba(120,60,220,.22);white-space:nowrap;
> 313	  will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 314	.chip .mk{width:40px;height:40px;border-radius:12px;display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;
> 315	  background:linear-gradient(150deg,#00e5ff,#7c48ff)}
> 316	.chip .t1{font-size:15.5px;font-weight:700}
> 317	.chip .t2{font-size:13px;opacity:.75;margin-top:3px}
> 318	.badge{position:absolute;width:62px;height:62px;border-radius:50%;
> 319	  background:rgba(18,10,38,.92);border:2px solid rgba(172,126,255,.9);
> 320	  box-shadow:0 0 28px rgba(150,90,255,.75), 0 0 0 7px rgba(124,72,255,.10);
> 321	  display:flex;align-items:center;justify-content:center;will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 322	.badge svg{width:27px;height:27px;fill:none;stroke:#dcc8ff;stroke-width:1.7}
> 323	.dataviz,.splitbar,.timeline{position:absolute;will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 324	.dttl{text-align:center;font-size:16px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.2em;text-transform:uppercase;
> 325	  color:#9fd8ff;margin-bottom:20px}
> 326	.plot{display:flex;align-items:flex-end;justify-content:center}
> 327	.col{position:relative;border-radius:20px 20px 5px 5px}
> 328	.col .glow{position:absolute;inset:-16px -12px -6px;border-radius:30px;filter:blur(22px);opacity:.9}
> 329	.col .body{position:absolute;inset:0;border-radius:20px 20px 5px 5px}
> 330	.blab{position:absolute;left:50%;bottom:100%;margin-bottom:14px;transform:translateX(-50%);
> 331	  text-align:center;white-space:nowrap;height:66px}
> 332	.blab .n{font-size:44px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.025em;line-height:1}
> 333	.blab .k{font-size:15px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.18em;text-transform:uppercase;opacity:.9;margin-top:7px}
> 334	.dcap{text-align:center;margin-top:26px;font-size:14.5px;letter-spacing:.05em;color:#93aac4}
> 335	.sbtrack{position:relative;display:flex;border-radius:18px;overflow:hidden;
> 336	  box-shadow:0 24px 70px rgba(0,0,0,.7), 0 0 40px rgba(124,72,255,.18)}
> 337	.sbL,.sbR{display:flex;flex-direction:column;align-items:center;justify-content:center;min-width:0}
> 338	.sbL{background:linear-gradient(180deg,#3b2f66,#241a45)}
> 339	.sbR{background:linear-gradient(180deg,#8fe8ff,#0092c4)}
> 340	.sbgap{width:0px;background:#04050a}
> 341	.sbv{font-size:30px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.02em}
> 342	.sbk{font-size:12.5px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.2em;text-transform:uppercase;opacity:.85;margin-top:4px}
> 343	.sbR .sbv,.sbR .sbk{color:#04222e}
> 344	.tlrow{display:flex;align-items:center;gap:18px;margin-top:22px}
> 345	.tllab{width:170px;text-align:right;font-size:14px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.08em;color:#a9c2d6}
> 346	.tlbar{position:relative;flex:1;border-radius:9px;background:rgba(140,195,255,.10);
> 347	  box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 1px rgba(140,195,255,.16)}
> 348	.tlmark{position:absolute;top:50%;width:10px;height:26px;margin:-13px 0 0 -5px;border-radius:4px;
> 349	  background:linear-gradient(180deg,#8fe8ff,#00b8e6);box-shadow:0 0 18px rgba(0,215,255,.8);opacity:0}
> 350	.tlcount{width:64px;font-size:22px;font-weight:800;color:#8fe8ff}
> 351	.counter{position:absolute;text-align:center;will-change:transform,filter,opacity;white-space:nowrap}
> 352	.counter .cv{font-size:92px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.03em;line-height:1;
> 353	  background:linear-gradient(92deg,#ffffff 28%,#7fe6ff);-webkit-background-clip:text;background-clip:text;color:transparent}
> 354	.counter .ck{font-size:16px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.22em;text-transform:uppercase;color:#9fd8ff;margin-top:12px}
> 355	.stamp{position:absolute;text-align:center;padding:26px 44px;border-radius:22px;white-space:nowrap;
> 356	  background:rgba(16,10,34,.86);border:2px solid rgba(178,120,255,.95);
> 357	  box-shadow:0 0 44px rgba(140,70,255,.6), inset 0 0 30px rgba(120,60,220,.25);
> 358	  will-change:transform,filter,opacity}
> 359	.stamp .sv{font-size:46px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.02em;color:#fff}
> 360	.stamp .ssrc{font-size:13px;letter-spacing:.16em;text-transform:uppercase;color:#9fd8ff;margin-top:10px;opacity:.9}
> 361	#hud{position:absolute;inset:0;pointer-events:none}
> 362	#scrim{position:absolute;${scrimCss}}
> 363	#kick{position:absolute;left:72px;top:56px;font-size:12.5px;letter-spacing:.34em;font-weight:700;color:#7fe6ff;text-transform:uppercase}
> 364	#kick b{color:#fff}
> 365	#rule{position:absolute;left:72px;top:84px;height:2px;background:linear-gradient(90deg,#00e5ff,rgba(0,229,255,0));border-radius:2px}
> 366	#title{position:absolute;left:72px;top:104px;width:${Math.round(W * 0.55)}px}
> 367	#title .l1{font-size:44px;font-weight:300;letter-spacing:-.02em;line-height:1.1;color:#cfe6f5}
> 368	#title .l2{font-size:58px;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:-.035em;line-height:1.06;
> 369	  background:linear-gradient(92deg,#ffffff 28%,#7fe6ff);-webkit-background-clip:text;background-clip:text;color:transparent}
> 370	#cap{position:absolute;left:72px;bottom:56px;font-size:15px;letter-spacing:.04em;color:#a9c2d6;width:${Math.round(W * 0.56)}px}
> 371	#cap span{color:#7fe6ff}
> 372	#end{position:absolute;left:72px;bottom:56px;width:640px}
> 373	#end .e1{font-size:14.5px;letter-spacing:.26em;font-weight:700;color:#7fe6ff;text-transform:uppercase}
> 374	#end .e2{font-size:30px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:-.02em;color:#eaf6ff;margin-top:12px;line-height:1.2}
> 375	#end .e3{font-size:14px;color:#8fa7bd;margin-top:13px;letter-spacing:.03em}
> 376	#vig{position:absolute;inset:0;box-shadow:inset 0 0 210px 66px rgba(0,0,0,.66)}
> 377	</style></head><body>
> 378	<div id="wash"></div>
> 379	<div id="weave"></div>
> 380	<div id="stage"><div id="cam">
> 381	${planesHtml}
> 382	</div></div>
> 383	<div id="hud">
> 384	  <div id="scrim"></div>
> 385	  ${HUD.kick ? `<div id="kick">${HUD.kick}</div>` : ""}
> 386	  <div id="rule" style="width:0px"></div>
> 387	  ${(HUD.title1 || HUD.title2) ? `<div id="title">${HUD.title1 ? `<div class="l1">${esc(HUD.title1)}</div>` : ""}${HUD.title2 ? `<div class="l2">${esc(HUD.title2)}</div>` : ""}</div>` : ""}
> 388	  ${HUD.caption ? `<div id="cap">${HUD.caption}</div>` : ""}
> 389	  ${HUD.end ? `<div id="end">${HUD.end.e1 ? `<div class="e1">${esc(HUD.end.e1)}</div>` : ""}${HUD.end.e2 ? `<div class="e2">${HUD.end.e2}</div>` : ""}${HUD.end.e3 ? `<div class="e3">${esc(HUD.end.e3)}</div>` : ""}</div>` : ""}
> 390	  <div id="vig"></div>
> 391	</div>
> 392	<script>
> 393	(function(){
> 394	  var C=${JSON.stringify(RT)};
> 395	  var P=C.P, D=C.D;
> 396	  function ease(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:1-Math.pow(1-x,3); }
> 397	  function smooth(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:x*x*(3-2*x); }
> 398	  function spring(x){ return x<=0?0:x>=1?1:1-Math.exp(-6*x)*Math.cos(9*x); }
> 399	  function clamp(v,a,b){ return v<a?a:v>b?b:v; }
> 400	  function seg(t,a,b){ return clamp((t-a)/(b-a),0,1); }
> 401	  function win(t,a,b,c,d){ return ease(seg(t,a,b))*(1-smooth(seg(t,c,d))); }
> 402	
> 403	  // A trilha e [t,x,y,z] em toda parte (o parser normaliza o legado [t,z] para x=0,y=0).
> 404	  function camAt(t){
> 405	    var K=C.CAM, L=K[K.length-1];
> 406	    for(var i=0;i<K.length-1;i++){
> 407	      if(t<=K[i+1][0]){
> 408	        var a=K[i], b=K[i+1];
> 409	        var u=smooth((t-a[0])/(b[0]-a[0]));
> 410	        return { x:a[1]+(b[1]-a[1])*u, y:a[2]+(b[2]-a[2])*u, z:a[3]+(b[3]-a[3])*u };
> 411	      }
> 412	    }
> 413	    return { x:L[1], y:L[2], z:L[3] };
> 414	  }
> 415	  function camZ(t){ return camAt(t).z; }
> 416	  // foco viaja entre estacoes [t, planeId] — mesma interpolacao do benchmark
> 417	  function focusZ(t){
> 418	    var S=C.focus.stations;
> 419	    var f=C.planes[S[0][1]];
> 420	    for(var i=1;i<S.length;i++){
> 421	      var a=S[i][0], b=a+0.8;
> 422	      f += (C.planes[S[i][1]]-f)*smooth(seg(t,a,b));
> 423	    }
> 424	    return f;
> 425	  }
> 426	
> 427	  var cam=document.getElementById('cam');
> 428	  var weaveEl=document.getElementById('weave');
> 429	  var planeEls={};
> 430	  [].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('[data-plane]')).forEach(function(el){ planeEls[el.getAttribute('data-plane')]=el; });
> 431	  var leafEls={};
> 432	  [].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('[data-leaf]')).forEach(function(el){ leafEls[el.getAttribute('data-leaf')]=el; });
> 433	  var kickEl=document.getElementById('kick'),ruleEl=document.getElementById('rule');
> 434	  var titleEl=document.getElementById('title'),capEl=document.getElementById('cap');
> 435	  var endEl=document.getElementById('end'),scrimEl=document.getElementById('scrim');
> 436	  var fmt=new Intl.NumberFormat('pt-BR',{maximumFractionDigits:6});
> 437	
> 438	  var vids=[].slice.call(document.querySelectorAll('video[data-vid]'));
> 439	  function syncVideos(t){
> 440	    var ps=vids.map(function(v){
> 441	      var d=v.duration;
> 442	      if(!isFinite(d)||d<=0) return Promise.resolve();
> 443	      var vt=t%d;
> 444	      if(Math.abs(v.currentTime-vt)<0.02) return Promise.resolve();
> 445	      return new Promise(function(res){
> 446	        var done=false;
> 447	        function ok(){ if(done)return; done=true; v.removeEventListener('seeked',ok); res(); }
> 448	        v.addEventListener('seeked',ok,{once:true});
> 449	        v.currentTime=vt;
> 450	        setTimeout(ok,300);
> 451	      });
> 452	    });
> 453	    return Promise.all(ps);
> 454	  }
> 455	
> 456	  async function seek(t){
> 457	    if(t<0)t=0; if(t>D)t=D;
> 458	    var cam3=camAt(t), cz=cam3.z, fz=focusZ(t);
> 459	    // trilha autorada + respiro senoidal (aditivo). Trilha zerada = comportamento antigo.
> 460	    var dx=cam3.x+Math.sin(t*0.40)*C.DRIFT_X, dy=cam3.y+Math.cos(t*0.31)*C.DRIFT_Y;
> 461	    cam.style.transform='translate3d('+dx.toFixed(2)+'px,'+dy.toFixed(2)+'px,'+cz.toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 462	
> 463	    var zs=[]; for(var pk in C.planes) zs.push(C.planes[pk]);
> 464	    var zBack=Math.min.apply(null,zs);
> 465	    if(weaveEl){
> 466	      var wEff=cz+zBack, wS=P/(P-wEff);
> 467	      weaveEl.style.transform='translate('+(dx*wS).toFixed(2)+'px,'+(dy*wS).toFixed(2)+'px) scale('+wS.toFixed(4)+')';
> 468	    }
> 469	    for(var pk2 in C.planes){
> 470	      var pel=planeEls[pk2];
> 471	      if(pel) pel.style.transform='translate3d(-50%,-50%,'+C.planes[pk2]+'px)';
> 472	    }
> 473	    // FOCO MOTIVADO — a camera entra na conta.
> 474	    // Antes: dof(z)=|z-fz|/k, so a distancia em Z de CENA. cz estava aqui no escopo, tres
> 475	    // linhas acima, e nunca era usado — entao a camera avancava 380px no dolly e a profundidade
> 476	    // de campo nao mudava um pixel. Opticamente impossivel, e o olho percebe sem saber nomear.
> 477	    // Agora a metrica e a distancia OPTICA ate a camera, como numa lente de verdade: o que esta
> 478	    // perto do plano focal fica nitido, e o desfoque cresce mais rapido no que esta perto da
> 479	    // camera do que no que esta longe — que e a assimetria que faz o quadro parecer fotografado.
> 480	    // cz cai para 1 quando a formula degenera (camera no plano de projecao), preservando o
> 481	    // comportamento antigo em vez de estourar em Infinity.
> 482	    function dof(z){
> 483	      if(!C.focus.motivated) return clamp(Math.abs(z-fz)/C.focus.k,0,C.focus.maxBlur);
> 484	      var dz=P-(cz+z), df=P-(cz+fz);
> 485	      if(dz<=1||df<=1) return C.focus.maxBlur;
> 486	      return clamp(C.focus.k*Math.abs(1/df-1/dz)*P,0,C.focus.maxBlur);
> 487	    }
> 488	
> 489	    var gIn=smooth(seg(t,0,C.fades.in)), gOut=smooth(seg(t,D-C.fades.out,D));
> 490	    var G=gIn*(1-gOut);
> 491	
> 492	    for(var li=0;li<C.layers.length;li++){
> 493	      var L=C.layers[li], el=leafEls[String(L.i)];
> 494	      if(!el) continue;
> 495	      var op=win(t,L.in[0],L.in[1],L.out[0],L.out[1])*G;
> 496	      el.style.opacity=String(op);
> 497	      el.style.filter='blur('+dof(L.z).toFixed(2)+'px)';
> 498	      var tr='';
> 499	      if(L.kind==='slab'&&L.rotY){
> 500	        var open=(L.rotY.base||0)+(L.rotY.open||0)*smooth(seg(t,(L.rotY.openWin||[0,D])[0],(L.rotY.openWin||[0,D])[1]))
> 501	          +Math.sin(t*(L.rotY.wobbleFreq||0.5))*(L.rotY.wobble||0);
> 502	        tr='rotateY('+open.toFixed(2)+'deg)';
> 503	        if(L.approach) tr+=' translateZ('+((1-ease(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])))*-L.approach).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 504	      } else if(L.spring){
> 505	        var sIn=Math.min(spring(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])),1.06);
> 506	        var parts=[];
> 507	        if(L.rotZ) parts.push('rotateZ('+L.rotZ+'deg)');
> 508	        if(L.rotY&&typeof L.rotY==='number') parts.push('rotateY('+L.rotY+'deg)');
> 509	        if(L.slideX) parts.push('translateX('+((1-sIn)*L.slideX).toFixed(1)+'px)');
> 510	        if(L.slideY) parts.push('translateY('+((1-sIn)*L.slideY).toFixed(1)+'px)');
> 511	        if(L.kind==='badge'||L.kind==='stamp') parts.push('scale('+sIn.toFixed(3)+')');
> 512	        tr=parts.join(' ');
> 513	      } else if(L.approach){
> 514	        tr='translateZ('+((1-ease(seg(t,L.in[0],L.in[1])))*-L.approach).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 515	      }
> 516	      // A centragem PREFIXA todo transform (e e escrita mesmo quando nao ha animacao) — senao o
> 517	      // primeiro transform do runtime apagaria o translate(-50%,-50%) do CSS e a camada saltaria
> 518	      // meia-caixa no meio da cena.
> 519	      if(L.ctr) el.style.transform='translate(-50%,-50%) '+tr;
> 520	      else if(tr) el.style.transform=tr;
> 521	
> 522	      if(L.bars){
> 523	        for(var b=0;b<L.bars.length;b++){
> 524	          var BR=L.bars[b];
> 525	          var bel=el.querySelector('[data-bar="'+L.i+':'+BR.bi+'"]');
> 526	          if(!bel) continue;
> 527	          var grow=Math.min(spring(seg(t,BR.grow[0],BR.grow[1])),1.06);
> 528	          bel.style.height=(BR.barMax*(BR.v/BR.max)*Math.max(grow,0)).toFixed(1)+'px';
> 529	          bel.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,BR.grow[0]-0.1,BR.grow[0]+0.6)));
> 530	        }
> 531	      }
> 532	      if(L.split){
> 533	        var sp=smooth(seg(t,L.split.at[0],L.split.at[1]));
> 534	        var lw=100-(100-L.split.lv)*sp, rw=L.split.rv*sp;
> 535	        var lel=el.querySelector('[data-sb="'+L.i+':L"]'), rel=el.querySelector('[data-sb="'+L.i+':R"]');
> 536	        var gel=el.querySelector('[data-sb="'+L.i+':G"]');
> 537	        if(lel) lel.style.width=lw.toFixed(2)+'%';
> 538	        if(rel) rel.style.width=rw.toFixed(2)+'%';
> 539	        if(gel) gel.style.width=(sp*6).toFixed(1)+'px';
> 540	        if(rel) rel.style.opacity=String(sp);
> 541	      }
> 542	      if(L.tl){
> 543	        for(var r=0;r<L.tl.length;r++){
> 544	          var TR=L.tl[r];
> 545	          for(var m=0;m<TR.marks;m++){
> 546	            var mel=el.querySelector('[data-tl="'+L.i+':'+TR.ri+':'+m+'"]');
> 547	            if(mel) mel.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,TR.from+m*TR.step,TR.from+m*TR.step+0.4)));
> 548	          }
> 549	          var cel=el.querySelector('[data-tlc="'+L.i+':'+TR.ri+'"]');
> 550	          if(cel) cel.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,TR.from+TR.marks*TR.step,TR.from+TR.marks*TR.step+0.5)));
> 551	        }
> 552	      }
> 553	      if(L.cnt){
> 554	        var cvEl=el.querySelector('[data-cv="'+L.i+'"]');
> 555	        if(cvEl){
> 556	          var u2=ease(seg(t,L.cnt.win[0],L.cnt.win[1]));
> 557	          var val=L.cnt.from+(L.cnt.to-L.cnt.from)*u2;
> 558	          cvEl.textContent=L.cnt.prefix+fmt.format(Number(val.toFixed(L.cnt.decimals)))+L.cnt.suffix;
> 559	        }
> 560	      }
> 561	    }
> 562	
> 563	    var hudOut=1-smooth(seg(t,C.hud.outWin[0],C.hud.outWin[1]));
> 564	    if(kickEl){ kickEl.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,0.1,0.8))*hudOut); }
> 565	    if(ruleEl){ ruleEl.style.width=(210*ease(seg(t,0.25,1.3))).toFixed(1)+'px'; ruleEl.style.opacity=String(hudOut); }
> 566	    if(titleEl){
> 567	      titleEl.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,0.45,1.5))*hudOut);
> 568	      titleEl.style.transform='translateY('+((1-ease(seg(t,0.45,1.6)))*24).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 569	    }
> 570	    if(capEl){ capEl.style.opacity=String(ease(seg(t,1.7,2.6))*hudOut); }
> 571	    var eOn=0;
> 572	    if(endEl){
> 573	      eOn=ease(seg(t,C.hud.endWin[0],C.hud.endWin[1]));
> 574	      endEl.style.opacity=String(eOn);
> 575	      endEl.style.transform='translateY('+((1-eOn)*18).toFixed(1)+'px)';
> 576	    }

Looking at how the `layerHtml` function processes field values, I'm checking where each parameter gets used. The `h` value is parsed but not actually applied to the cutout element—it only uses the x and y positioning along with the width, leaving height as auto.

For chip layers, the function reads the x, y, icon path, and text fields but ignores width and height since chips have intrinsic dimensions. The patch data for `cifra` and `cifra_src` layers only specifies x, y, and output values, which makes sense since both are center-aligned kinds that position using left and top.

Now I'm checking the plane references—the patch proposes adding a `host2` plane with a depth value, but I need to verify it's being added to the correct location in the spec structure, not nested under camera. The patch container is misleading — those keys belong at the top level of `spec`, not nested under `camera`. I need to restructure it with explicit top-level keys to avoid the engine ignoring them entirely. For the atmosphere nebulae, they only render when `horizon` is true AND the plane matches `horizonPlane`, so I'm verifying that `grid` exists in the planes list and checking the `horizonY` offset value.

Now I'm tracing through how nebulae are positioned — they use absolute positioning with `left` and `top` set to their top-left corners, not centers, so the actual nebula center is offset by half the width and height. For Encenador's nebula with center coordinates 420/860/1330 and dimensions 900×900, that puts the center at -1465 horizontally and -170 vertically on the grid plane.

Working through the camera projection math, the scale factor depends on the effective depth (camera z-position plus the plane's z-offset of -4200), and screen coordinates are calculated by translating the plane center through this scale plus any camera drift. The camera itself is transformed with `translate3d(dx, dy, cz)` inside the perspective container, with each plane as a child of the camera getting its own translation. is the projected screen position formula where the perspective scaling factor depends on the depth Z. Now I need to verify the layout dimensions against the canvas size configuration and understand how the coordinate system maps when perspective scaling is applied — the prompt specifies coordinates fit within 1720x720 centered at the origin, but perspective distortion means x values can actually exceed those bounds in layout space.

Let me track down the scene specification files and canvas dimensions to work through the actual math with real values.

## 🤖 Claude *(23:00:07)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

Looking at the base spec now and checking the onsets file for the actual anchor times. I notice the spec doesn't have a `hud` key at all, so HUD defaults to an empty object. The scrim is always rendered unconditionally, and the default value for `RT.hud.scrim` is "left" — this confirms what was claimed about the scrim behavior. I'm also checking what the default is for `hud.outWin`.

The scrim opacity calculation shows it fades out by 3.8 seconds when there's no HUD end value. And `hud.scrim: "bottom"` is indeed read from the spec, so that's valid.

Now I'm looking at the critical issue with `patchCamera` naming — the encenador nested `planes`, `focus`, `atmosphere`, `hud`, and `camera` under a single "patchCamera" object, but in the actual spec these are all top-level fields. Also, `negativeSpace` isn't read by the engine at all; it's just a Partitura documentation field. I'm verifying this by checking what the engine actually reads: `spec.aspect`, `duration`, `camera` properties, `planes`, `focus` settings, `layers`, `hud` options, `atmosphere` settings, and fade timing — but no `negativeSpace`.

Let me search through the spec reads to confirm, then check the timing onsets and work through the math for the camera perspective and screen positioning.

With W=1720 and H=720, the perspective origin at "50% 47%" gives me ox=860 and oy=338.4. The perspective distance P defaults to 1300. Camera keyframes define position offsets with some sinusoidal motion applied. When the camera moves to z=340 and a plane sits at z=-2300, the effective depth becomes -1960, which scales screen coordinates by about 0.399—moving the camera forward makes planes appear larger, a dolly-in effect.

For screen positioning, I'm calculating sx and sy by applying the camera's translated position (dx, dy) plus the plane's local coordinates (px, py), all scaled by the perspective factor. The plane's origin in the camera's local space is at (860, 360) since it's centered on the stage with its own centering transform applied. The numbers they provided don't quite match my calculations—they're showing 571.9 versus 616.4 for the host2 feet position. Looking at their critical finding, the cutout element uses absolute positioning with left and top, and the cutwrap applies a negative 50% translate transform to center itself, so the element's center ends up at the specified (x, y) coordinates.

I'm also noticing that the height parameter isn't actually used for the cutout rendering—only the width defaults to 300px when not specified. So their observation about the host with h:868 but no width rendering at 300px width is correct. I need to check the actual PNG dimensions to figure out what the implied heights should be, and I should verify whether the runtime transform properly overwrites or combines with the centering transform for the cutout element.

The spring default includes cutout, so the slideY default would be 0 unless explicitly authored—but the current spec does author slideY for cutouts. Since patches merge onto existing layer objects rather than replacing them entirely, I need to be careful that src and kind aren't lost in the process. Also confirming that chip kind ignores width and height parameters, which aligns with how the spec is authored.

For the focus.stations check, they must reference existing planes with a hard exit if they don't, and the host2 plane needs to be added. The plane order matters for paint order since the DOM order follows the object key insertion order, and with preserve-3d transforms, the browser sorts by z-index when there are ties.

Regarding the 3D depth sorting — Chromium handles depth sorting of polygons within a preserve-3d context, so planes with larger Z values (closer to the camera) paint in front. The caneca at z=-1900 appearing in front of mic at z=-2050 is plausible given this 3D rendering behavior. Since the existing scenes already rely on this engine behavior, I'll treat it as expected rather than a spec field.

Now checking whether the engine reads custom fields like `_porque` and `_nota` — unknown fields are simply ignored without validation, so these don't affect the engine's behavior.

The key issue is `negativeSpace`, which isn't actually a motor field at all but belongs to the Partitura instead. If someone merges patchCamera into the spec, `negativeSpace` would be inert but still needs to be flagged as ignored.

For the grid claim — the token `grid` is actually a plane name in spec.planes and is read as a plane (it's the horizonPlane), so the encenador's statement needs clarification.

Now I'm checking the onsets and PNG dimensions.

</details>

The measurements for the encenador check out perfectly — the mic centroid offset and caneca centroid both match the claimed values exactly. However, I'm noticing something important that was overlooked: host2 actually has an ink offset of +74.5px, meaning the trimmed PNG isn't truly centered as assumed. The alpha bounding box starts at x=149 out of 677, which reveals a misalignment in the baseline assumption.

This translates to a visible offset of roughly +15.7px on screen for host2, which is a real, measurable error in their model. The encenador claimed to have compensated the centroids they measured, but they only addressed mic and caneca — host2 was left uncompensated despite having an offset magnitude (11.0%) nearly identical to the mic's (10.7%). So while not catastrophic, this is a genuine discrepancy in their own work that undermines their claim of having fully compensated the measured centroids. The actual ink center for host2 lands around x=1320 rather than the intended socket position.

Now I'm checking the visible heights across the scale ladder using the fracH values and computing what each element displays at t=12.23 with their respective scale factors.

The ratios work out to 1 : 0.842 : 0.548 : 0.123, which matches the encenador's claimed 1 : 0.85 : 0.54 : 0.12 almost perfectly — so the ladder claim holds, though the absolute pixel values differ because they were probably measured at each element's own focal beat rather than a single timestamp.

If I back-calculate stature from the host's visible height of 447.9px at 175cm, that gives 2.560 px/cm, which would put the mic at 95.8cm and caneca at 21.6cm — both slightly larger than their stated measurements of 82.5cm and 17.6cm respectively.

Now I'm checking the current broken state to demonstrate what happens without the width parameter: all four cutouts render at w=300 instead of their proper widths.

Looking at the visible heights at t=12.23, I get 350.9 for the host, 105.9 for the mic, and 81.5 for the caneca. The user's observation that the caneca and mic are nearly the same size checks out — the ratio is 0.77 — which confirms the defect is real and my diagnosis is correct. Now I need to consider how visible widths factor into collision detection.

I'm going to write a proper sweep script that renders each layer's on-screen ink rectangle at 0.2-second intervals, checks frame bounds, and detects pairwise overlaps when both elements have opacity above the threshold. To get accurate chip sizes, I should check if Playwright is available in this repo and use it to render the patched spec in a real browser, then measure the actual bounding rectangles at each beat — that would give me definitive proof and catch the chip dimensions too.

The trickiest part is the merge itself—the patch's patchCamera contains a camera object that would completely replace the spec's camera and destroy the keyframes if applied naively, so I need to handle that field-by-field merge very carefully.

I'm spotting a critical timing issue—the patch isn't updating the cifra's input range, so at its focal beat of 15.34 seconds, the window function evaluates to zero instead of keeping it alive, which breaks the entire timing chain. Looking at the timing more carefully around the 15-16 second mark and also checking the earlier anchor at 1.77 seconds. At 1.77, the host element has opacity 0 due to the fade-in window starting at that exact point, so nothing appears on screen for the first word "simples" — it only becomes visible about 1.2 seconds later. This contradicts the claim that focus lands on the spoken word; the engine's window function starts at 0 opacity at the onset, meaning the element begins fading in *after* the word is spoken rather than landing on it. The same issue appears throughout: if you want an element to actually appear *at* the word, the window needs to account for this delay.

Now I'm seeing a second critical pattern — at each enumeration beat, the chip being named is completely invisible. At 27.1 seconds, chip2 isn't alive yet; at 30.03, only chip2 is visible while chip1 has zero opacity; at 33.06, chip3 is invisible while the others appear. So the named element is always dark at its own onset.

There's also a mismatch with what the encenador described about cifra's positioning. At 27.1, chip2 has zero opacity but cifra is fully opaque with no blur — the focus interpolation hasn't kicked in yet since it takes 0.8 seconds to transition. This means the frame shows an enormous sharp cifra (972 by 160 pixels) with nothing else visible, contradicting the stated layout.

The scale calculation confirms the discrepancy: at plane -820 with camera z at 1120, the scale factor is 1.30, making the 92px font render at roughly 120px tall. The text "R$ 100.000.000" spans 972 pixels horizontally — over half the frame width — and is centered at coordinates that place it far below where chip2 would appear, not 7.4 pixels behind it as claimed.

At the transition around t=28-29, both the cifra and chip2 coexist briefly while fading, but the collision detection shows no overlap between them. The real concern emerges when considering the camera's z-position approaching 1320: the cifra at plane -820 would reach a scale of 1.625 by t=41.1, and any object whose depth approaches the camera's far limit risks either exploding in scale or clipping behind the view entirely.

Now I'm noticing that mic and caneca sit dangerously low in the frame—mic's ink base at y=666 and caneca at y=681 when the frame height is 720. The collision happens around t=14, and while the sweep confirms nothing goes out of bounds, the margins are razor-thin. The encenador's measurement notes caneca at roughly 48 pixels from the screen edge, which translates to about 17.6 cm of actual ink involved.

Looking at the actual ink dimensions, mic measures 257×257 pixels with full visible height, while host is 167×453. Comparing the height ratios—host to host2 to mic to caneca—gives me 1 : 0.843 : 0.567 : 0.126, which matches the encenador's claimed 1 : 0.85 : 0.54 : 0.12 almost exactly. That confirms the ladder measurements. Using host as 175 cm and working backward through the pixel-to-centimeter conversion, mic comes out to about 99.3 cm and caneca to 22.0 cm, though the encenador had those at 82.5 cm and 17.6 cm—roughly a 20% discrepancy in their implied stature calculations.

Now I'm noticing something critical with the blur saturation. At t=12.23, both mic and caneca blur are maxed at 10.0, and that's exactly when the focus station switches to mic. But the focus transition from host2 (at 9.65) to mic takes 0.8 seconds, so at 12.23 the focal plane is still anchored to host2's depth while mic is already at maximum blur.

Let me trace back to the base version to understand what's changed. In the original, at 12.23 the mic blur was 0.0 because the focus station was chip1, not mic. The depth gap between chip1 and mic divided by the blur constant gives 5.8, which would clamp to the measured 0.0. So this maxed-out blur at the protagonist's own beat is a direct consequence of the patch's depth positioning—specifically the 1100-pixel Z gap between host2 and mic with the current blur constant. Their optical-weight formula actually inverts the dominance relationship — host2 should dominate by a huge margin (50,806 vs 546) but doesn't in the visual output. The root cause is the 0.8-second lag in the focus system: it arrives at the station well after the beat, and combined with the massive z-depth gap between host2 and mic, this creates an intermediate state where everything gets heavily blurred. I should verify this by checking if mic sharpens 0.8 seconds after the beat, and I've also confirmed the patch keeps the layer count at 4 maximum, whereas the base version has 7 at the same timestamp.

Now looking at the collision detection — the patched version shows only caneca and mic overlapping at 2898 square pixels at t=14, which is close to the predicted 2206 square pixels of intentional occlusion. But this overlap only appears during the fade-out window when both elements are disappearing, not during their main on-screen presence from 9.65 to 13.4 seconds.

Let me trace through the actual coordinates at 12.23 seconds to verify whether they're actually overlapping there too. The mic element spans from 685.5 to 942.5 horizontally and 409.5 to 666.5 vertically, while caneca spans 699 to 761 horizontally and 624.5 to 681.5 vertically — that's a 2604 square pixel overlap. So they do collide during their main display time, but my collision sweep only reported t=14 as the peak with a single sample, which contradicts this. I need to check the collision counting logic to understand why it's not capturing this earlier overlap. The DOM order and Z-depth are consistent across all planes—caneca renders in front of the mic as expected, and the chips are ordered by ascending Z values. At station 15.34, the focus targets cifra when nothing's visible there, which I've already noted. Now I need to check what's happening with the camera assignment. ## 🤖 Claude *(23:07:50)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm noticing a critical difference between the two versions: PATCHED has a blank frame around the 15-second mark during that specific lyric, where the screen goes completely empty except for the background elements, while BASE maintains continuous content throughout that same section.

The opacity issue is even more severe in the patched version — the protagonist should appear at full opacity on most of those anchor words, but instead it's invisible at six out of seven beats and heavily blurred at the seventh. The timing function is triggering the appearance too late, pushing the visual onset roughly a second after the word is actually spoken, which directly contradicts the core principle that the beat should land exactly on the spoken word.

The solution is to shift the input window so meaningful opacity exists right at the onset moment — something like starting the fade 0.55 seconds before and ending 0.35 seconds after gives nearly full opacity when the word hits, or at minimum pulling the window back so the full presence aligns with the word itself. There's a nuance with the host element at 1.77 where the fade timing works out differently, but the pattern holds.

On the blur side, the patch has cranked saturation way up — the protagonist and cifra are both maxed at 10.0 blur while invisible, and the host is also at maximum blur with zero opacity. The baseline had these at much lower values (2.6 to 9.5), and now there's a massive depth spread across the Z-axis that's making the visual hierarchy feel off.

The real problem is that the focus plane is still traveling when the beat hits, so everything sits in maximum blur for nearly a second while the focus catches up. The encenador's suggestion to push blur even higher would make this worse, so the fix is either to get the focus to arrive earlier or to compress the depth range and adjust the blur falloff curve. The measured peak of 2898 is close to their reported 2206, and it's a persistent overlap rather than a brief crossing, so that checks out. The diagonal ordering is working correctly now with the chips appearing in the right sequence, but they're rendering far too small relative to the frame—only about 10-11% of the width and the text is barely legible at 13 pixels on screen.

The `style` field on the t1 element is available and could control sizing, but the encenador didn't use it—that's a missed lever they could have pulled. The t2 element can't be styled at all, so enlarging the chips would require either using `style.size` on t1 or moving the plane closer. Let me systematically check what fields are actually available in the template to see what other options exist. Checking atmosphere.weave at line 281, horizon and horizonPlane at 227, horizonY at 287, nebulae at 228, and hud.scrim flowing into the scrim CSS at 258-260. Also confirming camera.keyframes at 78-80. For the unused properties—negativeSpace, _porque, _nota, staging, grid tokens, emphasis, anchor, groundShadow—none appear anywhere in the code, and hud.scrim:"none" just falls through to the left branch of the ternary since only "bottom" triggers the bottom styling.

Let me verify I haven't missed any reads of h or negativeSpace with a final grep.

## 🤖 Claude *(23:10:27)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

So `pos` only appears at the slab line, meaning `h` isn't actually used anywhere in the gancho scene since there are no slabs. I've confirmed that `negativeSpace`, `staging`, `emphasis`, `anchor`, and `groundShadow` are never read either. Now I'm ready to put together the final output showing which fields the engine actually uses.

Looking at the valid fields: `patchLayers` reads `plane`, `x`, `y`, and `w` (though `w` only applies to cutout layers), plus `in` and `out`. The camera settings pull from `planes`, `focus` with its nested `stations`, `k`, and `maxBlur`, `atmosphere` with `weave`, `horizon`, `horizonPlane`, `horizonY`, and `nebulae`, and the HUD's `scrim`.

For the invalid fields, `h` on cutout layers is completely dead since cutout doesn't use it anywhere, and `w` on the chip layers wasn't actually authored in the scene anyway.

The real problem is `patchCamera.camera` — it only contains a note, but when merged it overwrites `spec.camera` and destroys the `keyframes` array, causing the camera to silently fall back to a hardcoded default path. Fields like `negativeSpace`, the various `_nota` annotations, and the staging tokens like `grid`, `emphasis`, `anchor`, and `groundShadow` have zero references in the engine.

For collisions, the measurement found only one actual overlap between the caneca and mic elements, but the real issues are deeper: six of the seven main elements start at zero opacity when they appear, there's a completely empty frame at 15.0–15.4 seconds, the mic reaches maximum blur at its own beat, and the host2 element has uncompressed ink data.

Now I'm deciding how to structure these findings in the schema — the `colisoes` field expects strings, so I need to decide whether to list only geometric overlaps or include the temporal and rendering conflicts too, since those are equally important to the parent agent's understanding.

I'll include both types of conflicts in `colisoes` but prefix the temporal ones explicitly so they're clearly distinguished, then cover everything in the text response as well.
